\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},\mathbf{case}\,y^{c}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}@d_{1})\mathbf{msg}(y^{c}_{\alpha}.k;x_{1}^{c}\leftarrow y_{\alpha}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},\mathbf{case}\,y^{c}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}@d_{1})\mathbf{msg}(y^{c}_{\alpha}.k;x_{1}^{c}\leftarrow y_{\alpha}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(x_{1}^{c},[x_{1}^{c}/y^{c}_{\alpha}]P_{k}@c)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}

We consider sub-cases based on relevancy of process offering along
y^{c}_{\alpha}
. Observe that
y^{c}_{\alpha}
is relevant if an only if
x_{1}^{c}
is relevant, since they share a message of secrecy
c
.

Subcase 1.
\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},\mathbf{case}\,y^{c}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}@d_{1})
is not relevant.
By definition either
d_{1}\sqcup c=c\not\sqsubseteq\xi
or none of the channels connected to
P
including its offering channel
y_{\alpha}^{c}
are relevant. In both cases, means that
x_{1}^{c}
is not relevant and
\mathbf{msg}(y^{c}_{\alpha}.k;x_{1}^{c}\leftarrow y_{\alpha}^{c})
is not relevant either.
Moreover the continuation process
\mathbf{proc}(x_{1}^{c},[x_{1}^{c}/y^{c}_{\alpha}]P_{k}@c)
won’t be relevant. And

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(x_{1}^{c},[x_{1}^{c}/y^{c}_{\alpha}]P_{k}@c)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi

Subcase 2.
\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},\mathbf{case}\,y^{c}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}@d_{1})
is relevant.

By definition of relevancy, we get that
c\sqcup d_{1}=c\sqsubseteq\xi
and thus
y^{c}_{\alpha}
is relevant. This means that
\mathbf{msg}(y^{c}_{\alpha}.k;x_{1}^{c}\leftarrow y_{\alpha}^{c})
is relevant too.
By assumption that
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
, and definition of
=_{\xi}
:

\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},\mathbf{case}\,y^{c}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow{P_{\ell}}_{\mid\xi})_{\ell\in L}@d_{1})\mathbf{msg}(y^{c}_{\alpha}.k;x_{1}^{c}\leftarrow y_{\alpha}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2},

such that
{P_{\ell}}_{\mid\xi}
is equal to
P_{\ell}
modulo renaming of some channels with secrecy level higher than or incomparable to the observer.

We have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(x_{1}^{c},[x_{1}^{c}/y^{c}_{\alpha}]{P_{k}}_{\mid\xi}@c)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}}

This completes the proof of the subcase as we know that the relevancy of channels in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{i}
remain intact.

Case 7.
\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\eta}^{c}y_{\alpha}^{c};y_{\alpha}^{c}\leftarrow x_{1}^{c})\mathbf{proc}(v^{c^{\prime}}_{\eta},w\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}y^{c}_{\alpha};P@d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\eta}^{c}\,y_{\alpha}^{c};y_{\alpha}^{c}\leftarrow x_{1}^{c})\mathbf{proc}(v^{c^{\prime}}_{\eta},w\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}y^{c}_{\alpha};P@d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(v^{c^{\prime}}_{\eta},[x^{c}_{\eta}/w][x_{1}^{c}/y^{c}_{\alpha}]P@c\sqcup d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}

We consider sub-cases based on relevancy of process offering along
v^{c^{\prime}}_{\eta}
.

Subcase 1.
\mathbf{proc}(v^{c^{\prime}}_{\eta},w\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}y^{c}_{\alpha};P@d_{1})
is not relevant.
By definition either
d_{1}\sqcup c\not\sqsubseteq\xi
or none of the channels connected to
P
including
y_{\alpha}^{c}
are relevant.

In both cases by the definition of quasi running secrecy we know that neither
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\eta}^{c}y_{\alpha}^{c};x_{1}^{c}\leftarrow y_{\alpha}^{c})
nor the continuation process
\mathbf{proc}(v^{c^{\prime}}_{\eta},[x^{c}_{\eta}/w][x_{1}^{c}/y^{c}_{\alpha}]P@c\sqcup d_{1})
are relevant. It is then straightforward to see that

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(v^{c^{\prime}}_{\eta},[x^{c}_{\eta}/w][x_{1}^{c}/y^{c}_{\alpha}]P@c\sqcup d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi.

Subcase 2.
\mathbf{proc}(v^{c^{\prime}}_{\eta},w\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}y^{c}_{\alpha};P@d_{1})
is relevant.

By definition of relevancy, we get that
c\sqcup d_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
. This implies that
y^{c}_{\alpha}
is relevant in the pre-step. From relevancy of
y^{c}_{\alpha}
and the quasi running secrecy lower than or equal to the observer of the positive message
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\eta}^{c}\,y_{\alpha}^{c};y_{\alpha}^{c}\leftarrow x_{1}^{c})
we get that the message is relevant too.
By assumption:

\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\eta}^{c}\,y_{\alpha}^{c};y_{\alpha}^{c}\leftarrow x_{1}^{c})\mathbf{proc}(u^{c^{\prime}}_{\gamma},w\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}y^{c}_{\alpha};P_{\mid\xi}@d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2},

such that
{P_{\ell}}_{\mid\xi}
is equal to
P_{\ell}
modulo renaming of some channels with secrecy level higher than or incomparable to the observer.

We have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(u^{c^{\prime}}_{\gamma},[x^{c}_{\eta}/w][x_{1}^{c}/y^{c}_{\alpha}]{P}_{\mid\xi}@d_{1}\sqcup c)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}}

We need to consider that the quasi running secrecy of the process may increases in the post step based on the code of
P
and
P_{\mid_{\xi}}
. The argument for this case is similar to the previous cases of the proof. See
Case 1.
Subcase 2.
. One interesting situation is when the relevancy of chain of positive and relevant messages in the pre-step of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}
changes in the post-step. By relevancy in the pre-step we know that these chains exist in both runs, so the same chain of messages will become irrelevant in the post-step of both runs.

Case 8.
\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},w\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}y^{c}_{\alpha};P@d_{1})\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\eta}^{c}y_{\alpha}^{c};x_{1}^{c}\leftarrow y_{\alpha}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},w\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}y^{c}_{\alpha};P@d_{1})\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\eta}^{c}y_{\alpha}^{c};x_{1}^{c}\leftarrow y_{\alpha}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(x_{1}^{c},[x^{c}_{\eta}/w][x_{1}^{c}/y^{c}_{\alpha}]P@c\sqcup d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}

We consider sub-cases based on relevancy of process offering along
y^{c}_{\alpha}
.

Subcase 1.
\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},w\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}y^{c}_{\alpha};P@d_{1})
is not relevant.
By definition either
d_{1}\sqcup c=c\not\sqsubseteq\xi
or none of the channels connected to
P
including
y_{\alpha}^{c}
are relevant. In both cases the negative message
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\eta}^{c}y_{\alpha}^{c};x_{1}^{c}\leftarrow y_{\alpha}^{c})
and the continuation process
\mathbf{proc}(x_{1}^{c},[x^{c}_{\eta}/w][x_{1}^{c}/y^{c}_{\alpha}]P@c\sqcup d_{1})
are not relevant either. It is then straightforward to see that

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(x_{1}^{c},[x^{c}_{\eta}/w][x_{1}^{c}/y^{c}_{\alpha}]P@c\sqcup d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi.

Subcase 2.
\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},w\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}y^{c}_{\alpha};P@d_{1})
is relevant.

By definition of relevancy, we get that
c\sqcup d_{1}=c\sqsubseteq\xi
and
y^{c}_{\alpha}
is relevant. This means that
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\eta}^{c}\,y_{\alpha}^{c};x_{1}^{c}\leftarrow y_{\alpha}^{c})
and the channel and
x^{c}_{\eta}
are relevant. By assumption that
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
, and definition of
=_{\xi}
:

\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},w\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}y^{c}_{\alpha};P_{\mid\xi}@d_{1})\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\eta}^{c}\,y_{\alpha}^{c};x_{1}^{c}\leftarrow y_{\alpha}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2},

such that
{P}_{\mid\xi}
is equal to
P
modulo renaming of some channels with secrecy level higher than or incomparable to the observer.

We have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(x_{1}^{c},[x^{c}_{\eta}/w][x_{1}^{c}/y^{c}_{\alpha}]{P}_{\mid\xi}@d_{1}\sqcup c)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}}

The proof is similar to previous cases.

Case 9.
\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},y^{c}_{\alpha}\leftarrow x^{c}_{\beta}@d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},y^{c}_{\alpha}\leftarrow x^{c}_{\beta}@d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}[x^{c}_{\beta}/y^{c}_{\alpha}]\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}

We consider sub-cases based on relevancy of process offering along
y^{c}_{\alpha}
. Observe that
y^{c}_{\alpha}
is relevant if and only if
x^{c}_{\beta}
is relevant.

Subcase 1.
\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},y^{c}_{\alpha}\leftarrow x^{c}_{\beta}@d_{1})
is not relevant.
By definition either
c\not\sqsubseteq\xi
or none of the channels
y_{\alpha}^{c}
and
x_{\beta}^{c}
are relevant. In both cases, it means that
y_{\alpha}^{c}
and
x_{\beta}^{c}
are not relevant. As a result, we can safely make the substitution
[x^{c}_{\beta}/y^{c}_{\alpha}]
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
. If
c\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, it is only the matter of renaming channels with secrecy level higher than or incomparable to the observer level. And if
c\sqsubseteq\xi
, then
y_{\alpha}^{c}
does not occur in any relevant process and we can rename it to
x_{\beta}^{c}
.

Moreover, deleting
\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},y^{c}_{\alpha}\leftarrow x^{c}_{\beta}@d_{1})
from the configuration does not decrease the quasi-running secrecy of any of its (grand)children since by the tree invariant
d_{1}\sqsubseteq c
. In particular, a chain of positive messages offered along
x^{c}_{\beta}
, has its minimum quasi running secrecy of
d_{1}\sqsubseteq c=c
. Removing
\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},y^{c}_{\alpha}\leftarrow x^{c}_{\beta}@d_{1})
may increase the quasi running secrecy of such chain of messages. This case is only of significance if
c\sqsubseteq\xi
. By the assumption of subcase, we know that
x_{\beta}^{c}
is irrelevant, and thus the chain of messages have to be irrelevant in the pre-state.

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},y^{c}_{\alpha}\leftarrow x^{c}_{\beta}@d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}[x^{c}_{\beta}/y^{c}_{\alpha}]\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi

Subcase 2.
\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},y^{c}_{\alpha}\leftarrow x^{c}_{\beta}@d_{1})
is relevant.

By definition of relevancy, we get that
c\sqsubseteq\xi
and both
y^{c}_{\alpha}
and
x^{c}_{\beta}
are relevant. By assumption that
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
, and definition of
=_{\xi}
:

\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},y^{c}_{\alpha}\leftarrow x^{c}_{\beta}@d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}.

We have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}[x^{c}_{\beta}/y^{c}_{\beta}]\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}}

With the same reasoning as in
Subcase 1.
, the quasi running secrecy of a chain of messages offered along
x^{c}_{\beta}
is
c
and does not decrease after deleting the process. However, it may increase based on the quasi-running secrecy of the parents of
\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},y^{c}_{\alpha}\leftarrow x^{c}_{\beta}@d_{1})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{i}
. If the parent in one run has a quasi running secrecy lower than or equal to the observer level, then it has to be relevant and thus there is a counterpart in the other run with a quasi running secrecy lower than or equal to the observer level. Thus in both runs the relevancy of the chain of messages does not change in the post-step. If both of the parents have running secrecy higher than or incomparable to the observer level, then the same chain of messages become irrelevant in both runs.

Case 10.
\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},(x^{d}\leftarrow P)@d_{2};Q@d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},(x^{d}\leftarrow P)@d_{2};Q@d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(a^{d}_{0},[a^{d}_{0}/x^{d}]P@d_{2})\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},[a^{d}_{0}/x^{d}]Q@d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}

We consider sub-cases based on relevancy of process offering along
y^{c}_{\alpha}
.

Subcase 1.
\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},(x^{d}\leftarrow P)@d_{2};Q@d_{1})
is not relevant.
By definition either
d_{1}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
or none of the channels of this process including
y_{\alpha}^{c}
are relevant.

In both cases, it means that both
\mathbf{proc}(a^{d}_{0},[a^{d}_{0}/x^{d}]P@d_{2})
and
\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},[a^{d}_{0}/x^{d}]Q@d_{1})
are not relevant either. Note that
d_{1}\sqsubseteq d_{2}
and thus
d_{2}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
.

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(a^{d}_{0},[a^{d}_{0}/x^{d}]P@d_{2})\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},[a^{d}_{0}/x^{d}]Q@d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi

Subcase 2.
\mathbf{proc}(y^{c}_{\alpha},(x^{d}\leftarrow P)@d_{2};Q@d_{1})
is relevant.

By definition of relevancy, we get that
d_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
and all channels of this process with secrecy levels lower than or equal to the observer level are relevant. By assumption that
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
, and definition of
=_{\xi}
:

\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(z^{c}_{\delta},(x^{d}\leftarrow P_{\mid\xi})@d_{2};Q_{\mid\xi}@d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}.

We have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(a^{d}_{0},[a^{d}_{0}/x^{d}]P_{\mid\xi}@d_{2})\mathbf{proc}(z^{c}_{\delta},[a^{d}_{0}/x^{d}]Q_{\mid\xi}@d_{1})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}}

Remark:we can assume that the fresh channel being spawned will be
a
in both runs.

Note that if
a^{d}_{0}
has secrecy level lower than or equal to the observer level, then taking this step won’t change relevancy of any channels. Otherwise some resource of the process may become irrelevant after this step since
a^{d}_{0}
may block their relevancy path or in the case where
d_{2}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
the process becomes irrelevant. But this happens to the processes in the both runs. (similar to the cases 3 and 4 for
\otimes
and
\multimap
)

∎

Lemma
A.9
indicates how two related configurations
{(\mathcal{B}_{1},\mathcal{B}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket}
can be broken down into
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{i}}}
such that
\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{C}_{i}::\Delta
, and
\Psi;\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{i}::K
, and
\Psi;K\Vdash\mathcal{F}_{i}::\cdot
. Fig.
6
illustrates interesting key cases, indicating that trees rooted at non-observable channels are internalized into
\mathcal{D}_{i}
.

Lemma A.9

(Build a Meganode)
.

Consider
\mathcal{C}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}{\mathcal{F}_{1}}
, and
{\mathcal{C}_{2}}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}
such that
\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{C}_{1}::\Delta_{1}
and

\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{C}_{2}::\Delta_{2}
, and
\Psi;\Delta_{1}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{1}::x^{c}_{\alpha}{:}A
, and

\Psi;\Delta_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{2}::y^{d}_{\beta}{:}B
, and
\Psi;x^{c}_{\alpha}{:}A\Vdash\mathcal{F}_{1}::\cdot
, and

\Psi;y^{d}_{\beta}{:}B\Vdash\mathcal{F}_{2}::\cdot
.

If
\Delta_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\Delta_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi=\Delta
and
x^{c}_{\alpha}{:}A{\Downarrow}\xi=y^{d}_{\beta}{:}B{\Downarrow}\xi=K
, then we can rewrite
\mathcal{C}_{i}\mathcal{D}_{i}\mathcal{F}_{i}
as

\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{i}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{i}
such that

{({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}}})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi}(\Delta\Vdash K)}.

Moreover, if
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
, then
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
.

Proof.

We break down the proof into the following cases based on the structure of
K
and
\Delta
.

Case 1.

x^{c}_{\alpha}{:}A{\Downarrow}\xi=y^{d}_{\beta}{:}B{\Downarrow}\xi=K\neq\cdot
. This means that
x^{c}_{\alpha}{:}A=y^{d}_{\beta}{:}B
and
c\sqsubseteq\xi
. By the tree invariant, we know that
\Delta_{1}=\Delta_{2}=\Delta.
So without any rewrite configurations
\mathcal{C}_{i}\mathcal{D}_{i}\mathcal{F}_{i}
satisfy the properties that we are looking for.

Case 2.

x^{c}_{\alpha}{:}A{\Downarrow}\xi=y^{d}_{\beta}{:}B{\Downarrow}\xi=\cdot
, and
\Delta_{1}=\Lambda_{1},\Delta
, and
\Delta_{2}=\Lambda_{2},\Delta
. By typing of configurations we have
\mathcal{C}_{i}=\mathcal{T}_{i}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{i}
such that
\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{T}_{1}::\Delta
, and

\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1}::\Lambda_{1}
, and
\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{T}_{2}::\Delta
, and
\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{2}::\Lambda_{2}
.

From the definition of projections, we have
c\not\sqsubseteq\xi
and
d\not\sqsubseteq\xi
and for every
u^{c^{\prime}}_{\gamma}\in\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}
, we know that
c^{\prime}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
.
We build
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}=\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{i}\mathcal{D}_{i}\mathcal{F}_{i}
, and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{i}=\mathcal{T}_{i}
, and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{i}=\cdot
. By the typing rules, we know that
\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{i}::\cdot
and
\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{T}_{i}::\Delta
and
\Psi;\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}::\cdot
as we need to establish

{({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}}})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi}(\Delta\Vdash K)}.

Moreover, by the definition of projections, since
c\not\sqsubseteq\xi
and
d\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, we know that none of the processes in
\mathcal{F}_{i}
will be relevant in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}
. Also, adding
\mathcal{F}_{1}
as a parent of the process/message offering along
x^{c}_{\alpha}
, does not switch relevancy of any process in
\mathcal{D}_{1}
, since we already know that
c\not\sqsubseteq\xi
. In particular if we have a message offering along
x^{c}_{\alpha}
, its quasi running secrecy is higher than or incomparable to the observer level before adding
\mathcal{F}_{1}
as its parent and will stay higher than or incomparable to the observer level after too. The same reasoning goes with
\mathcal{F}_{2}
and
y^{c}_{\beta}
.

Similarly, since
c^{\prime}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
for every
u^{c^{\prime}}_{\gamma}\in\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}
, and the fact that
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{i}
does not use any resources in
\Delta
, we know that all trees in
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{i}
are irrelevant. As a result, we have
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
.

∎

Fig. 6:
Schematic illustration of Lemma
A.9
. Observable channels are depicted in green.

Lemma A.10

(Diamond Property)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}\in\mathsf{Tree}(\Delta\Vdash K)
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{1}}}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{1}}}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}
then there is a configuration
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}}}

Proof.

The proof is straightforward by cases.
∎

Confluence of multistep reduction follows by two standard inductions from the diamond property.

Lemma A.11

(Forward closure)
.

If
({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket
, and
({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi}(\Delta\Vdash K)
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{1}}}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}
then

({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket
.

Proof.

By definition of
\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
, we know that
({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi}(\Delta\Vdash K),
and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{1}}}\mapsto^{*}_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}\,\mathsf{and}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}}\mapsto^{*}_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}.

By confluence, we have

\star\;{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}\mapsto^{*}_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}.

By Thm.
A.5
, we get

\star^{\prime}\;({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi}(\Delta\Vdash K).

\star
and
\star^{\prime}
completes the proof.
∎

Lemma A.12

(Backward Closure)
.

If
({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket
, and
({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi}(\Delta\Vdash K)
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{1}}}
then

({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket
.

Proof.

By definition of
\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
, we know that
({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi}(\Delta\Vdash K),
and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{1}}}\mapsto^{*}_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}\,\mathsf{and}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}}\mapsto^{*}_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}.

By definition of
\mapsto^{*}_{\Delta\Vdash K}
, we have

\star\;{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}\mapsto^{*}_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{1}}}\mapsto^{*}_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}\,\mathsf{and}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}}\mapsto^{*}_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}.

\star
along with the assumption of
({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{2}}})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi}(\Delta\Vdash K)
completes the proof.
∎

Theorem A.13

(Fundamental Theorem)
.

For all security levels
\xi
, and configurations
{\Psi;\Delta_{1}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{1}::u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}T_{1}}
and
{\Psi;\Delta_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{2}::v_{\beta}^{c_{2}}{:}T_{2}}
with
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
,
\Delta_{1}\Downarrow\xi=\Delta_{2}\Downarrow\xi
, and
u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}T_{1}\Downarrow\xi=v_{\beta}^{c_{2}}{:}T_{2}\Downarrow\xi
we have

(\Delta_{1}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{1}::u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}T_{1})\equiv^{\Psi}_{\xi}(\Delta_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{2}::v_{\beta}^{c_{2}}{:}T_{2}).

Proof.

Put
\Delta=\Delta_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\Delta_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
, and
K=u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}T_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=v_{\beta}^{c_{2}}{:}T_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
. The goal is to prove that for all
\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2}
and
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}
such that

{\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{C}_{1}::\Delta_{1}}

and

{\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{C}_{2}::\Delta_{2}}

and

{\Psi;u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}{:}T_{1}\Vdash\mathcal{F}_{1}::\cdot}

and

{\Psi;v^{c_{1}}_{\beta}{:}T_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{F}_{2}::\cdot}

\;\dagger\;(\mathcal{C}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{F}_{1};\mathcal{C}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket.

The proof is by induction on a lexicographic order on (a) the size of types
\Delta,K
and (b) the multiset
\mathcal{M}
of derivations
\Psi;\Delta^{\prime}\vdash P::y_{\gamma}{:},[d]{T^{\prime}}
used in the derivations of
\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{C}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{F}_{1}::\cdot
and
\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{C}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{2}::\cdot
. Derivations are ordered in the standard way and we use the multiset ordering derived from this as the basis of our induction, using
<
to present it.

By Lemma
A.9
we can rewrite
\mathcal{C}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{F}_{1}
as
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{C}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{2}
as
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}
such that
{({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}}})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi}(\Delta\Vdash K)}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
.

By progress, either 1) at least one of the configurations can take a step, i.e.
{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}}
or
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}}}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}
such that
({\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{i}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{i}}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{i}})<{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{i}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{i}}
in the multiset ordering, or 2)
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}}}
are poised with regard to
\Delta\Vdash K
, or 3) both
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}
are empty with
\Delta=K=\cdot
.

The proof of Case 3) is trivial by definition. We consider 1) and 2) separately. Here, without loss of generality, we only consider the cases for
{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}}
and
{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}}
being poised.

1.

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}\mapsto_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}
:

By Lemma
A.8
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}}}\mapsto^{0,1}_{\Delta\Vdash K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}
with

\Delta\Vdash{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}::K
, and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on
(\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2})<(\mathcal{C}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{2})
to get

\star\;\;(\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket.

By Backward closure lemma (Lemma
A.12
):

\dagger\;\;(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{C}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket.

2.

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}}}
are both poised with regard to
\Delta\Vdash K
:

Case 1.

K=u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}T_{1}=u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}1
, and
\Delta=\cdot
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\cdot}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha})}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}
.
Note that by the definition of relevancy, all processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
are relevant.
As a result,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}),
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}=\cdot
.
By line 1 of the definition, we get

\dagger\,({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\cdot\Vdash u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}{:}1\rrbracket
.

Case 2.

K=u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}T_{1}=u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I},
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}.k).

By the assumption of the theorem,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
are both relevant, and we have
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}.k;u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}).

Removing
\mathbf{msg}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}.k)
from
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
does not change relevancy of the remaining configuration when
c_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
:

\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on
\dagger_{1}
and
\star_{2}
and the smaller type
K
to get

\begin{array}[]{l}{{\color[rgb]{0.18,1,0.7}}\star}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{msg}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}.k;u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}})\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{msg}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}.k;u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}})\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})}}\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash w^{c_{1}}_{\gamma}{:}A_{k}\rrbracket.\end{array}

By line (2) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}.k;u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}})\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}.k;u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}})\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}\oplus\{\ell:A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}\rrbracket.\end{array}

Case 3.

K=u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}T_{1}=u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}\&\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{msg}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}.k;w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha})\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}
.

We consider two subcases:

Subcase 1.
{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}\neq\mathbf{msg}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}.k;w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha})\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}.

By line (3) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}.k;w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha})\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}\&\{\ell:A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}\rrbracket.\end{array}

Subcase 2.
{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathbf{msg}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}.k;w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha})\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller type
K
, but first we need to show that the invariant of the induction holds. From
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
and
c_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
, we get

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}.k;w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}){\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}.k;w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}){\Downarrow}\xi.

By induction hypothesis

\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0.18,1,0.7}\star}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}.k;w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha})}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}.k;w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha})}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2})}}\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash w^{c_{1}}_{\gamma}{:}A_{k}\rrbracket.\end{array}

By line (3) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}.k;w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha})\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}.k;w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha})\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}\&\{\ell:A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}\rrbracket.\end{array}

Case 4.

K=y_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}T_{1}=u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}A\otimes B
, and

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{c_{1}}\,u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}};u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}})

where
\Delta=\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}
and
\Psi;\Lambda_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{T}_{1}::(x_{\beta}^{c_{1}}{:}A)
.

By assumption of the theorem and
c_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
, we know
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
are both relevant and

{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{c_{1}}\,u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}};u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}),}

such that
\Psi;\Lambda_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{T}_{2}::(x_{\beta}^{c_{1}}{:}A)
.Moreover, by relevancy of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}
(and relevancy of
x_{\beta}^{c_{1}}
) we get
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
and

\mathcal{T}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{T}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
.

By configuration typing we can break down
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{i}
into

and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{i}
such that
\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{H}_{i}::\Lambda_{1}
, and
\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{i}::\Lambda_{2}
.
In the case where
x^{c_{1}}_{\beta}{:}A\in\Delta
, we have
\mathcal{T}_{i}=\cdot
and
\Lambda_{2}=x^{c_{1}}_{\beta}{:}A
.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller type to get

\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0.18,1,0.7}\star^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{T}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{H}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{c_{1}}\,u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}};u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}})\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{T}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{c_{1}}\,u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}};u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}})\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})}}\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Lambda_{2}\Vdash x^{c_{1}}_{\beta}{:}A\rrbracket.\end{array}

and

\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0.18,1,0.7}\star^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{H}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{c_{1}}\,u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}};u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}})\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{H}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{c_{1}}\,u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}};u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}})\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})}}\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Lambda_{1}\Vdash w^{c_{1}}_{\gamma}{:}B\rrbracket.\end{array}

By line (5) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{c_{1}}\,u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}};u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}})\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{c_{1}}\,u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}};u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}})\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}A\otimes B\rrbracket.\end{array}

Case 5.

K=u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}T_{1}=u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}A\multimap B

and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x^{c_{1}}_{\beta}\,u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha};w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}})\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}
.
There are two subcases to consider:

Subcase 1.

{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}\neq\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x^{c_{1}}_{\beta}\,u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha})\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2},}
. By line (4) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x^{c_{1}}_{\beta}\,u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha};w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}})\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}A\multimap B\rrbracket.\end{array}

Subcase 2.

{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x^{c_{1}}_{\beta}\,u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha})\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2},}
.

By assumption and
c_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
, we know that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
are relevant. Adding a negative message along
c_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
as the root does not change their relevancy:

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x^{c_{1}}_{\beta}\,u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}){\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x^{c_{1}}_{\beta}\,u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}){\Downarrow}\xi

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller type:

\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0.18,1,0.7}\star}\;\;({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x^{c_{1}}_{\beta}\,u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha};w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}})}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x^{c_{1}}_{\beta}\,u^{c_{1}}_{\gamma};w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}})}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2})}}\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta,x_{\beta}^{c_{1}}{:}A\Vdash w^{c_{1}}_{\gamma}{:}B\rrbracket.\end{array}

By line (4) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x^{c_{1}}_{\beta}\,u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha};w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}})\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x^{c_{1}}_{\beta}\,u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha};w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}\leftarrow u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}})\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}A\multimap B\rrbracket.\end{array}

Case 6.

K=u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}A
, and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha},u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}\leftarrow w^{c_{1}}_{\gamma}@d_{1}).

By assumption of the theorem,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha},u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}\leftarrow w^{c_{1}}_{\gamma}@d_{1}).

By assumption of the theorem,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
, since
w^{c_{1}}_{\gamma}
is relevant in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{i}
, and
we can apply the induction hypothesis on
\dagger_{1}
and
\star_{2}
and the smaller configuration with respect to
<
order to get

\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0.18,1,0.7}\star\;}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}/u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}]\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[w_{\gamma}^{c_{1}}/u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}]\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})}}\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash w^{c_{1}}_{\gamma}{:}A\rrbracket.\end{array}

By line (11) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
:

\hskip-14.22636pt\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha},u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}\leftarrow w^{c_{1}}_{\gamma}@d_{1})\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha},u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha}\leftarrow w^{c_{1}}_{\gamma}@d_{1})\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha}^{c_{1}}{:}A\rrbracket.\end{array}

Case 7.

\Delta=\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}^{c}{:}1
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{c}_{\gamma})}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}
.

There are two subcases to consider:

Subcase 1.

\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\neq\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{c}_{\gamma})
.

By line 6 in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
,

\dagger\,({\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{c}_{\gamma})}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},x^{c}_{\gamma}{:}1\Vdash K\rrbracket

Subcase 2.

\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{c}_{\gamma})
.

We first briefly explain why the invariant of induction holds after we bring the closing message inside
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}
and remove the channel
x_{\gamma}^{c}
from
\Delta
, i.e.

\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{c}_{\gamma})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{c}_{\gamma})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi.

If the parent of
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{c}_{\gamma})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
is relevant in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
before bringing the message insider then by the assumption of theorem, it is the same as the parent of
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{c}_{\gamma})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
. If after adding the message
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
the parent still remains relevant, it means that it has at least one other relevant channel other than
x^{c}_{\gamma}
in both
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
which also exists in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
and will be relevant after adding the message to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
. If after adding the message
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
becomes irrelevant, it means that it does not have a relevant path to any other channel in
\Delta^{\prime}
and
K
. By the assumption of theorem the parent of the message in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
does not have such path either. The same argument holds for any other node that becomes irrelevant because of adding the closing message to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}
. As a result the same processes becomes irrelevant in both
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
after adding the message to them and the proof of this case is complete. The same argument holds for the case in which the parent of
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{c}_{\gamma})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
before adding the message is relevant. Otherwise the parent of
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{c}_{\gamma})
is irrelevant in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
before adding the message and remains irrelevant after that too. The proof in this case is straightforward.

Now that the invariant holds, we can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller types:

{\color[rgb]{0.18,1,0.7}\star}\;({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{c}_{\gamma})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{c}_{\gamma})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime}\Vdash K\rrbracket.

By line 6 in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
,

\dagger\,({\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{c}_{\gamma})}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{c}_{\gamma})}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},x^{c}_{\gamma}{:}1\Vdash K\rrbracket

Case 8.

\Delta=\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}^{c}{:}\oplus\{\ell:A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\delta}^{c})}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}
.

By configuration typing, we have
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{i}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{i}
such that
\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{T}_{i}::\Delta^{\prime}
and
\Psi;\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{i}::x_{\gamma}^{c}{:}\oplus\{\ell:A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}
. We know that
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\delta}^{c})
.
We consider two subcases:

Subcase 1.
{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{2}\neq\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\delta}^{c})}
.

By line (7) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger(\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\delta}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}^{c}{:}\oplus\{\ell:A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\Vdash K\rrbracket.\end{array}

Subcase 2.

{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\delta}^{c})}
.

We have
\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\delta}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\delta}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
, since
w^{c}_{\delta}
is relevant and the positive messages
\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\delta}^{c})
in both configurations are relevant only if their parents are. Thus adding the message does not change relevancy of any other process.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller type to get

\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0.18,1,0.7}\star\;}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\delta}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\delta}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})}}\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},w^{c}_{\delta}:A_{k}\Vdash K\rrbracket.\end{array}

By line (7) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger(\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\delta}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\delta}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}^{c}{:}\oplus\{\ell:A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\Vdash K\rrbracket.\end{array}

Case 9.

\Delta=\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}^{c}{:}\&\{\ell:A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;w_{\delta}^{c}\leftarrow x^{c}_{\gamma})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}

By the assumption of the theorem,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;w_{\delta}^{c}\leftarrow x^{c}_{\gamma})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}.
Moreover,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
:
c\sqsubseteq\xi
and thus

remains relevant in
\mathcal{D}_{i}
and no relevancy changes in the configurations after removing the negative message.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller type to get

\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0.18,1,0.7}\star}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;w_{\delta}^{c}\leftarrow x^{c}_{\gamma})}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;w_{\delta}^{c}\leftarrow x^{c}_{\gamma})}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})}}\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},x^{c}_{\gamma+1}{:}A_{k}\Vdash K\rrbracket.\end{array}

By line (3) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;w_{\delta}^{c}\leftarrow x^{c}_{\gamma})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(x^{c}_{\gamma}.k;w_{\delta}^{c}\leftarrow x^{c}_{\gamma})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},x^{c}_{\gamma}{:}\&\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\Vdash K\rrbracket.\end{array}

Case 10.

\Delta=\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}^{c}{:}A\otimes B
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y_{\delta}^{c}\,x_{\gamma}^{c};x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\eta}^{c})}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}
.

We consider two subcases:

Subcase 1.

\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\neq\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y_{\delta}^{c}\,x_{\gamma}^{c};x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\eta}^{c})
.

By line (7) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger\;(\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y_{\delta}^{c}\,x_{\gamma}^{c};x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\eta}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}^{c}{:}A\otimes B\Vdash K\rrbracket.\end{array}

Subcase 2.

\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y_{\delta}^{c}\,x_{\gamma}^{c};x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\eta}^{c})
.

Moreover, we have

\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y_{\delta}^{c}\,x_{\gamma}^{c};x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\eta}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y_{\delta}^{c}\,x_{\gamma}^{c};x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\eta}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi:

The quasi-running secrecy of
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y_{\delta}^{c}\,x_{\gamma}^{c};x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\eta}^{c})
is lower than or equal to the observer level if the quasi-running secrecy of its parent is lower than or equal to the observer level. So the relevancy of the parent of the message and thus the rest of configurations do not change by adding the message to the configuration.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller type to get

\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0.18,1,0.7}\star\;}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y_{\delta}^{c}\,x_{\gamma}^{c};x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\eta}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y_{\delta}^{c}\,x_{\gamma}^{c};x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\eta}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})}}\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},y^{c}_{\delta}{:}A,w^{c}_{\eta}{:}B\Vdash K\rrbracket.\end{array}

By line (7) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger\;(\mathcal{A}_{1}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y_{\delta}^{c}\,x_{\gamma}^{c};x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\eta}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{A}_{2}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y_{\delta}^{c}\,x_{\gamma}^{c};x_{\gamma}^{c}\leftarrow w_{\eta}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}^{c}{:}A\otimes B\Vdash K\rrbracket.\end{array}

Case 11.

\Delta=\Delta^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime},x_{\gamma}^{c}{:}A\multimap B
. And
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y^{c}_{\delta}\,x^{c}_{\gamma};w_{\eta}^{c}\leftarrow x_{\gamma}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}}
, such that
\Psi;\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Vdash\mathcal{T}_{1}::y^{c}_{\delta}{:A}
and
\Psi;\Delta^{\prime},w^{c}_{\eta}{:B}\Vdash\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}::K
.

The message
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y^{c}_{\delta}\,x^{c}_{\gamma};w_{\eta}^{c}\leftarrow x_{\gamma}^{c})
is relevant in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
. By assumption of the theorem,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y^{c}_{\delta}\,x^{c}_{\gamma})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2},
such that
\Psi;\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Vdash\mathcal{T}_{2}::y^{c}_{\delta}{:A}
and
\Psi;\Delta^{\prime},w^{c}_{\eta}{:}B\Vdash\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}::K
.

By typing configuration, we have
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{i}=\mathcal{A}_{i}\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{i}\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{i}
such that
\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{A}_{i}::\Delta^{\prime}
and
\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{i}::\Delta^{\prime\prime},x^{c}_{\gamma}{:}A\multimap B
.

By assumption we know that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
. By definition of relevancy, we know that
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y^{c}_{\delta}\,x^{c}_{\gamma};w_{\eta}^{c}\leftarrow x_{\gamma}^{c})
and the tree
\mathcal{T}_{1}
connected to it are relevant in
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and thus
\mathcal{T}_{1}
is equal to
\mathcal{T}_{2}
in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
. Removing these from both configurations does not change relevancy of the rest of the configuration since
w^{c}_{\eta}
will remain relevant, and the relevancy of the message’s parent does not change:
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller type to get

\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0.18,1,0.7}\star\,}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{T}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{A}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y^{c}_{\delta}\,x^{c}_{\gamma};w_{\eta}^{c}\leftarrow x_{\gamma}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{T}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{A}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y^{c}_{\delta}\,x^{c}_{\gamma};w_{\eta}^{c}\leftarrow x_{\gamma}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})}}\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Vdash y^{c}_{\delta}{:}A\rrbracket.\end{array}

and

\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0.18,1,0.7}\star^{\prime}\,}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{A}_{1}\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y^{c}_{\delta}\,x^{c}_{\gamma};w_{\eta}^{c}\leftarrow x_{\gamma}^{c})}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{A}_{2}\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y^{c}_{\delta}\,x^{c}_{\gamma};w_{\eta}^{c}\leftarrow x_{\gamma}^{c})}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})}}\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},w^{c}_{\eta}{:}B\Vdash K\rrbracket.\end{array}

By line (10) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y^{c}_{\delta}\,x^{c}_{\gamma};w_{\eta}^{c}\leftarrow x_{\gamma}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y^{c}_{\delta}\,x^{c}_{\gamma};w_{\eta}^{c}\leftarrow x_{\gamma}^{c})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime}_{1},x^{c}_{\gamma}{:}A\multimap B\Vdash K\rrbracket.\end{array}

Case 12.
\Delta=\Delta^{\prime},x_{\delta}^{c}{:}A
.

\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(x^{c}_{\delta},x^{c}_{\delta}\leftarrow w^{c}_{\gamma}@d_{1}).

We consider two subcases:

Subcase 1.

\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\neq\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(x^{c}_{\delta},x^{c}_{\delta}\leftarrow w^{c}_{\gamma}@d_{1})
.
By line (12) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
:

\hskip-14.22636pt\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(x^{c}_{\delta},x^{c}_{\delta}\leftarrow w^{c}_{\gamma}@d_{1})\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{C}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta,x^{c}_{\delta}{:A}\Vdash K\rrbracket.\end{array}

Subcase 2.

\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(x^{c}_{\delta},x^{c}_{\delta}\leftarrow w^{c}_{\gamma}@d_{1})
.

By assumption of the theorem, we have
[w^{c}_{\gamma}/x^{c}_{\delta}]\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=[w^{c}_{\gamma}/x^{c}_{\delta}]\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
, since we just rename a relevant channel in both configurations. We can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller typing judgment of the multiset to get

\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0.18,1,0.7}\star\;}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{[w_{\gamma}^{c}/u_{\delta}^{c}]\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{[w_{\gamma}^{c}/x_{\delta}^{c}]\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})}}\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},x^{c}_{\delta}{:}A\Vdash K\rrbracket.\end{array}

By line (12) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi}
:

\hskip-14.22636pt\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(x^{c}_{\delta},x^{c}_{\delta}\leftarrow w^{c}_{\gamma}@d_{1})\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(x^{c}_{\delta},x^{c}_{\delta}\leftarrow w^{c}_{\gamma}@d_{1})\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta,x^{c}_{\delta}{:A}\Vdash K\rrbracket.\end{array}

∎

\hskip 18.49988pt\hookrightarrow_{\Delta\Vdash y^{c}_{\alpha}{:}A}{\mathsf{queue}^{\prime}\{x/y\}}&\mathsf{and}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[x^{c}_{\beta}/y^{c}_{\alpha}]\mathcal{F}}}\hookrightarrow_{\Delta\Vdash x^{c}_{\beta}{:}A}{\mathsf{queue}^{\prime}}\\
(13)\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{proc}(x^{c^{\prime}}_{\beta},x^{c^{\prime}}_{\beta}\leftarrow z^{c^{\prime}}_{\gamma}@d_{1})}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}_{1}}}&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{proc}(x^{c^{\prime}}_{\beta},x^{c^{\prime}}_{\beta}\leftarrow z^{c^{\prime}}_{\gamma}@d_{1})}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\mathcal{D}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{F}}}\in\mathsf{Tree}(\Delta,x^{c^{\prime}}_{\beta}{:}A\Vdash K)\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\hookrightarrow_{\Delta,x^{c^{\prime}}_{\beta}{:}A\Vdash K}{\mathsf{queue}^{\prime}\overline{\{x/y\}}}&\mathsf{and}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{[z^{c^{\prime}}_{\gamma}/x^{c^{\prime}}_{\beta}]\mathcal{D}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[z^{c^{\prime}}_{\gamma}/x^{c^{\prime}}_{\beta}]\mathcal{F}}}\hookrightarrow_{\Delta,z^{c^{\prime}}_{\gamma}{:}A\Vdash K}{\mathsf{queue}^{\prime}}\\
(14)\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\cdot}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\cdot}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\cdot}}\hookrightarrow_{\cdot\Vdash\cdot}\cdot&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\cdot}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\cdot}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\cdot}}\in\mathsf{Tree}(\cdot\Vdash\cdot)\end{array}

Fig. 7:
Definition of
\hookrightarrow_{\Delta\Vdash K}
. An overline indicates that a message is sent from
\mathcal{C}
or
\mathcal{F}
to
\mathcal{D}
, otherwise the message is sent from
\mathcal{D}
to
\mathcal{C}
or
\mathcal{F}
.

A-A

Examples Typing

\mathsf{auth}

mer

=

\binampersand\{

\mathit{tok_{1}}{:}\oplus\{\mathit{succ}{:}\,\mathsf{account}\otimes 1,\mathit{fail}{:}\,1\},

\hskip 9.24994pt\vdots

\mathit{tok_{n}}{:}\oplus\{\mathit{succ}{:}\,\mathsf{account}\otimes 1,\mathit{fail}{:}\,1\}\}

\mathsf{customer}

=

\mathsf{auth}\multimap 1

\mathsf{account}

=

\oplus\{\mathit{high}{:}1,\,\mathit{med}{:}1,\,\mathit{low}{:}1\}

\mathsf{rate}

=

\&\{\mathit{lowRate}{:}1,\,\mathit{highRate}{:}1\}

Must-Type-Check Process Definitions

\cdot\vdash\mathsf{Alice}::y{:}\,\mathsf{customer}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}[\mathbf{alice}]}

y\leftarrow\mathsf{Alice}\leftarrow\cdot=(

//
\cdot\vdash y{:}\mathsf{customer}[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

w\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}\,y;

//
w{:}\mathsf{auth}[\mathbf{alice}]\vdash y{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

w.\mathit{tok}_{j};

//
w{:}\oplus\{\mathit{succ}{:}\,\mathsf{account}\otimes 1,\mathit{fail}{:}\,1\}[\mathbf{alice}]\vdash y{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

\mathbf{case}\,w\,(

\mathit{succ}\Rightarrow

//
w{:}\mathsf{account}\otimes 1[\mathbf{alice}]\vdash y{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

v\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}\,w;

//
v{:}\mathsf{account}[\mathbf{alice}],w{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]\vdash y{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

\mathbf{case}\,v\,(

\mathit{high}\Rightarrow

//
v{:}1[\mathbf{alice}],w{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]\vdash y{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

\mathbf{wait}\,v;

//
w{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]\vdash y{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

\mathbf{wait}\,w;

//
\cdot\vdash y{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

\mathbf{close}\,y

\mid\mathit{med}\Rightarrow

\mathbf{wait}\,v;

\mathbf{wait}\,w;

\mathbf{close}\,y

\mid\mathit{low}\Rightarrow

\mathbf{wait}\,v;

\mathbf{wait}\,w;

\mathbf{close}\,y)

\mid\mathit{fail}\Rightarrow

//
w{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]\vdash y{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

\mathbf{wait}\,w;

//
\cdot\vdash y{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

\mathbf{close}\,y)){\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}@\mathbf{alice}}

u{:}\,\&\{\mathit{s}{:}\mathsf{account},\mathit{f}{:}1\}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}[\mathbf{alice}]}\vdash\mathsf{aAuth}::x{:}\mathsf{auth}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}[\mathbf{alice}]}

x\leftarrow\mathsf{aAuth}\leftarrow u=(

//

\mathbf{case}\,x\,(

\mathit{tok}_{j}\Rightarrow

//
u{:}\,\&\{\mathit{s}{:}\mathsf{account},\mathit{f}{:}1\}[\mathbf{alice}]\vdash x{:}\oplus\{\mathit{succ}{:}\,\mathsf{account}\otimes 1,\mathit{fail}{:}\,1\}[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

x.\mathit{succ};

//

//

\mathbf{send}\,u\,x;

//
\cdot\vdash x{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

\mathbf{close}\,x

\mid\mathit{tok}_{i\neq j}\Rightarrow

//
u{:}\,\&\{\mathit{s}{:}\mathsf{account},\mathit{f}{:}1\}[\mathbf{alice}]\vdash x{:}\oplus\{\mathit{succ}{:}\,\mathsf{account}\otimes 1,\mathit{fail}{:}\,1\}[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

x.\mathit{fail};

//
u{:}\,\&\{\mathit{s}{:}\mathsf{account},\mathit{f}{:}1\}[\mathbf{alice}]\vdash x{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

u.\mathit{f};

//
u{:}\,1[\mathbf{alice}]\vdash x{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

\mathbf{wait}\,u;

//
\cdot\vdash x{:}1[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

\mathbf{close}\,x)){\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}@\mathbf{alice}}

\cdot\vdash\mathsf{aAcc}::u{:}\&\{\mathit{s}{:}\mathsf{account},\mathit{f}{:}1\}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}[\mathbf{alice}]}

u\leftarrow\mathsf{aAcc}\leftarrow\cdot=(

//
\cdot\vdash u{:}\,\&\{\mathit{s}{:}\mathsf{account},\mathit{f}{:}1\}[\mathbf{alice}]@\mathbf{alice}

x{:}\,\mathsf{auth}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}[\mathbf{alice}]},y{:}\,\mathsf{customer}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}[\mathbf{alice}]},x^{\prime}{:}\mathsf{auth}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}[\mathbf{bob}]},y^{\prime}{:}\,\mathsf{customer}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}[\mathbf{bob}]},u{:}\,\mathsf{rate}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}[\mathbf{guest}]}\vdash\mathsf{Bank}::z{:}1{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}[\mathbf{bank}]}

z\leftarrow\mathsf{Bank}\leftarrow x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime},u=(

//
x{:}\,\mathsf{auth}[\mathbf{alice}],y{:}\,\mathsf{customer}[\mathbf{alice}],x^{\prime}{:}\mathsf{auth}[\mathbf{bob}],y^{\prime}{:}\,\mathsf{customer}[\mathbf{bob}],u{:}\,\mathsf{rate}[\mathbf{guest}]\vdash z{:}1[\mathbf{bank}]@\mathbf{guest}

\mathbf{send}\,x\,y;

//
y{:}\,1[\mathbf{alice}],x^{\prime}{:}\mathsf{auth}[\mathbf{bob}],y^{\prime}{:}\,\mathsf{customer}[\mathbf{bob}],u{:}\,\mathsf{rate}[\mathbf{guest}]\vdash z{:}1[\mathbf{bank}]@\mathbf{guest}

note that

\mathbf{guest}\sqsubseteq\mathbf{alice}

\mathbf{send}\,x^{\prime}\,y^{\prime};

//
y{:}\,1[\mathbf{alice}],y^{\prime}{:}\,1[\mathbf{bob}],u{:}\,\mathsf{rate}[\mathbf{guest}]\vdash z{:}1[\mathbf{bank}]@\mathbf{guest}

note that

\mathbf{guest}\sqsubseteq\mathbf{bob}

u.\mathit{lowRate};

//
y{:}\,1[\mathbf{alice}],y^{\prime}{:}\,1[\mathbf{bob}],u{:}\,1[\mathbf{guest}]\vdash z{:}1[\mathbf{bank}]@\mathbf{guest}

note that

\mathbf{guest}\sqsubseteq\mathbf{guest}

\mathbf{wait}\,y;

//
y^{\prime}{:}\,1[\mathbf{bob}],u{:}\,1[\mathbf{guest}]\vdash z{:}1[\mathbf{bank}]@\mathbf{bank}

note that

\mathbf{guest}\sqcup\mathbf{alice}=\mathbf{bank}

\mathbf{wait}\,y^{\prime};

//
u{:}\,1[\mathbf{guest}]\vdash z{:}1[\mathbf{bank}]@\mathbf{bank}

note that

\mathbf{bob}\sqcup\mathbf{bank}=\mathbf{bank}

\mathbf{wait}\,u;

//
\cdot\vdash z{:}1[\mathbf{bank}]@\mathbf{bank}

note that

\mathbf{guest}\sqcup\mathbf{bank}=\mathbf{bank}

\mathbf{close}\,z){\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}@\mathbf{guest}}
